I haven't really noticed a change, if that's what you're asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel restless or fidgety?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Um... like if I'm sitting in a meeting, my leg is usually bouncing. Or I'll be clicking a pen... just shifting around in the chair. I don't really notice I'm doing it. I just sort of... end up messing with whatever is in front of me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose. Um... mainly with the paperwork. Like, I’ll finish an incident report and think it’s solid. But then the captain will pull me aside... points out I put the wrong unit number, or I missed a time stamp somewhere. Or I skipped some little box they need for the safety review. Just... yeah, that kind of stuff happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself talking more than others in social situations?\n\nPatient: I don't know. Not really, I guess. I just... talk when I need to. I don't think I talk more than anyone else.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're constantly on the go?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I guess... that's sort of why I'm here, actually. I just feel like I'm always running hot. Like I can't really... shut the motor off. And, um... lately, that's been getting me in trouble at the firehouse. So... yeah. I guess I do.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble staying seated in one place for a long time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess. It's... hard to explain. When we have command meetings and I have to just sit there, it feels like... I don't know, like my body is just revving too high. I just get this urge to stand up. Walk the bay, check the rigs... anything but stay planted in that chair. It's just... uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Were you more physically active or restless than your peers when you were a child?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess so. My mom always said I was like this back in grade school, too. I'd... get out of my seat a lot. Blurt things out before the teacher finished asking. I was always losing my homework, too... getting notes sent home. They said I was bright enough to do the work, I just... couldn't stay still long enough to actually do it. So, yeah. I guess it's been a while.\n\nDoctor: Did you have any problems with behavior or paying attention in school when you were younger?\n\nPatient: I think I just said that, but... yeah. Um... my mom always told me I was like this in grade school. Getting up, talking out of turn. Losing my homework. They told her I was smart enough, just... couldn't focus or sit still. So... yes.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have strong mood swings?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't really know what you mean by that. I guess I get frustrated sometimes, but... no more than anyone else, probably. I wouldn't call it swings. Just... normal stuff.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

I guess... yeah, it's been a while. My mom says I was like this back in grade school. Always out of my seat, blurting things out... losing my homework. Getting notes sent home. She said I was bright enough to do the work, I just... couldn't keep it together, I guess. So, yeah. Since I was a kid.
